ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u26	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour and pleasure on behalf of the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation, but also in my own, to present to you a draft law on investment in the construction or reconstruction of the Świnoujście watercourse - Szczecin to a depth of 12.5 meters. The Act sets out the conditions for the preparation and implementation of investments in infrastructure providing access to the port, consisting in the construction or conversion of the Świnoujście watercourse - Szczecin to a depth of 12.5 m, which are strategic for the economy and security of the State, and the sources of funding for this investment. Investments consisting of the construction or conversion of the Świnoujście watercourse - Szczecin to a depth of 12.5 m include robots, supplies or services consisting of: deepening the water track Szczecin - Świnoujście to a depth of 12.5 m with its expansion and geometry adjustments... [[Game in the O.R.]] Your Marshal, I'm very sorry, but it's very disturbing. I think that if the Members are not interested, they can talk in the roundabouts. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u27	Once again, ladies and gentlemen, please remain calm in the plenary room. You're welcome, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u28	...which consists of: deepening the Świnoujście watercourse - Szczecin to a depth of 12.5 m along with its extension and correction of track geometry, as well as the completion of the passing lanes and the deepening of the Grabowski Channel section between the Orla Przesmyk turntable and the turntable on the combination of the Grabowski Canal and the Dębicki Basin, the section of the water track between the Grain Basin and the turntable on the combination of Grabowski Canal and the Dębicki Basin; the level of the water track in the Świnoujście region; the deepening and expansion of the turntables: at the height of Poly together with the section of the water track in the Policki Canal to the waterfront in the Police Seaport, at the Orlim Pass in the Mieleński Basin at the entrance to the Grabowski Canal, at the combination of the Grabowski Canal and the Dębicki Basium; the construction of artificial islands in the Szczecin canal, together with the reconstruction and the construction of new or reconstruction of existing water-related fields; the comprehensive expansion of the Navigation Baza in Szczecin; the construction network or the construction zone, with the construction or reconstruction of the environment, with the construction of the environment, or reconstruction activities associated with the environment. The investment investor is the Director of the Maritime Office in Szczecin. Investments are to be financed from the State budget and the budget of the European Union, but this does not exclude financing from other sources. Investments are public objectives within the meaning of the Real Estate Management Act of 21 August 1997. If it is decided for the investment to determine the location of the public-purpose investment, and in the lines delimiting the area of the investment, the land constituting the self-certification parcels on the Management Board of the State Forest Farm, which is to be used for the purpose of the investment and, for forest land, excluded from production, the competent local fore-winders shall conclude a contract for free transfer of the management with the Director of the Maritime Office in Szczecin within 60 days of the date of receipt of the request for transfer of the Management Board, and shall transfer the land. If the land on the Management Board of State Forests does not constitute self-certification parcels, the competent local forward-looking land shall, within 30 days of receipt of the request for transfer of the Management Board, apply to the competent authority for the distribution of the property. Within 60 days of the date on which the decision to approve the division of the property became final or that decision was granted immediate feasibility, the competent local fortress shall conclude an agreement with the Director of the Maritime Office in Szczecin for the transfer of the management board free of charge and shall transfer those land. The costs of sharing the properties referred to above shall be borne by the Director of the Maritime Office in Szczecin. The Director of the Maritime Office in Szczecin, on the basis of a construction project for investment, shall specify in the application for transfer of the management board the land to be used for investment purposes and, for forestry land, excluded from production. Where the decision on the construction permit relating to the investment concerns family parcel gardens established under the Law of 13 December 2013 on family parcel gardens, the investor is obliged to: pay the landlords compensation for the property belonging to the parcels and owned by them for the rights to land in the family parcel garden, in the amount of the crop provided for in the case of the liquidation of the family parcel garden during the period 1 April to 31 October; to pay the garden association compensation for the property owned by the landowners, buildings and buildings of the family parcel garden intended for the joint use of the land and intended to ensure the operation of the garden, and to compensate for the costs and losses incurred in connection with the liquidation of the family parcel garden. If the investor has not determined the amount of compensation by decision of the West Pomeranian Voivode within 30 days of the date of issue of the building permit, by agreement with the interested parties, the compensation shall be determined by decision of the West Pomeranian Voivode. In the case of the implementation of the investment in the port area of Szczecin, the investor's obligations to the extent specified in the agreement concluded with the Director of the Maritime Office in Szczecin may be fulfilled by the managing body of the port, which is the owner or perpetual user of the property in which the family gardens are located. In the case referred to in the first sentence, the investor shall reimburse to the managing body of the port the costs of payment of compensation up to the amount specified in the estimated operation. The amount and payment of compensation in the cases referred to above shall be determined in accordance with the property management rules. And now I'm going to give you the reasons for this bill. The draft Act on investments in the construction or conversion of the Świnoujście watercourse - Szczecin to a depth of 12.5 metres was prepared to improve the preparation and implementation of the investment project on access to the seaport in Szczecin, Poland. The modernisation of the Świnoujście - Szczecin track to a depth of 12.5 metres included in the list of identified projects, as Annex 5 to the detailed descriptions of the priority axes of the Operational Programme for 2014-2020. The project is on this list under the number. 323, item 264. The entire procedure for the estimation of investment costs ensures financing and monitoring of expenditure, is carried out in a separate manner in accordance with European and national rules, including the Law of 27 August 2009 on public finances. This investment will be implemented within the framework of the Operational Programme "Infrastructure and Environment" for the period 2014-2020. The investment is strategic for the economy and state security. Urgent implementation of the modernization of the Świnoujście - Szczecin water track to a depth of 12.5 m is essential for the development of seaports of vital importance to the national economy and thus ensuring the safety of shipping on the water track. The provisions of the proposed law will apply to the entity involved in the implementation of the investment carried out under the law, i.e. to the Director of the Maritime Office in Szczecin. The proposed law will also apply to bodies carrying out environmental arrangements for investments and users of parcel gardens. The aim of the proposed law is to introduce procedural facilitations for the exemption of the investor and environmental decision-making body from the obligation to establish compliance with the spatial planning plan and land solutions on which family gardens are located, including the definition of a procedure for dealing with possible compensation for the seizure of those land during the implementation of the investment. The procedure laid down in the Act accompanying the liquidation of family gardens will simplify and speed up the acquisition of land necessary for the implementation of investments and will facilitate the amicable resolution of the problems involved. The investment is mainly carried out on the marine waters of the sea and on the sea shore, i.e. land in the permanent management board of the Maritime Office in Szczecin. Lands on which the gardens are located are located on the island of Ostrów Grabowski in Szczecin and are in perpetual use of the Board of Maritime Ports Szczecin and Świnoujście SA. The draft law takes into account the specificities of investments in waterways and the specific characteristics of the investor, which will only be the Director of the Maritime Office in Szczecin, which represents the Treasury. The aim of the special scheme is to introduce procedural facilitations for pre-investment preparatory work and not to provide financing for investments. The project is a continuation and the third stage of implementation of the Świnoujście - Szczecin water track modernisation project. The second stage, i.e. the project under the name "Modernisation of the Świnoujście watercourse - Szczecin, the Piast Canal and Małiński, the eastern and western side," was carried out in the financial perspective of 2007. The investment is not subject to the provisions of the Act of 3 October 2008 on the provision of environmental information and its protection, public participation in environmental protection and on environmental impact assessments, which require the establishment of environmental conditions for investment in compliance with the local spatial planning, due to the fact that not all municipalities in which the investment will take place have current spatial planning plans and that some of them may be incompatible with the local spatial planning plan. All other environmental issues will be resolved in accordance with the applicable legislation in the context of the environmental impact assessment under the environmental conditions decisions issued for investment, which ensures that investments are implemented in a manner consistent with environmental requirements. On 9 January 2017, a decision of the Minister for Development and Finance No 13/POIiŚ2020/2017 was issued to ensure the financing of the implementation of the project under the name "Modernisation of the Świnoujście - Szczecin watercourse to a depth of 12.5 m. A total of 26,552 000 will be included in the budget limit set for this purpose in the budget laws for the period 2017 and 2022. zł. The adoption of the Act is of key and strategic importance primarily for the West Pomeranian voivodship, creating the basis for the implementation of investments included in the Transport Development Strategy by 2020 and providing, in particular, for the efficient implementation of further infrastructure and industrial projects, namely: maintaining the conditions for operating and expanding the external port in Świnoujście by creating a LNG export position, expanding the port infrastructure in the southern part of the port in Świnoujście to enable the mooring and operation of ro-pax ferries up to 220 m long with the accompanying infrastructure, adapting the ferry terminal infrastructure in Świnoujście to intermodal transport, expanding the port infrastructure in the port in Szczecin by improving the availability of the waterfront with improved navigation conditions at the Dębicki Canal and the Kaszubski Basin, expanding the port in Police with the terminal of liquid chemicals in relation to the investment of Police 2, i.e. the state-of-of-the-art and largest installations in Europe for the production of propylene by the PDH method. This action is consistent with the European Union's priority, which places great emphasis on improving transport corridors via environmentally friendly modes of transport. The investment will ensure an increase in freight mass, which will pass through the Baltic - Adriatic corridor through ports in Świnoujście and Szczecin. The idea of creating motorways of the sea is linked to the need to develop alternative transport links for intermodal transport. From the organizational and investment side both the Management Board of Maritime Ports Szczecin and Świnoujście SA, the Swedish partners of the port as well as the ship owners operating on this line are prepared to handle additional freight. The Act, supporting economic functions in the transport, logistics and industrial field, provides conditions for port development by contributing to the TEN-T trans-European transport network programme co-financed by the European Investment Bank, the European Structural and Investment Funds and the Connecting Europe Facility. The aim is to improve the availability of seaports and coastal infrastructure while supporting maritime transport as an alternative to other modes of transport. As part of the public consultation which took place from 29 July to 1 August, the provisions concerning the modernization of the Świnoujście - Szczecin watercourse up to 12.5 m long were assessed by the West Pomeranian Voivodeship, the Marshal of the West Pomeranian Voivodeship, the Management Board of Maritime Ports Szczecin and Świnoujście SA and the Management Board of the Port of Police sp. z o.o. Ww. the authorities have not submitted any comments to the draft. The bill was also addressed to trade unions and employers' organizations, including the National Trade Union Agreement, NSZZ Ww. the entities also failed to comment on the draft law in question. The opinion of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs states that the draft law is in line with European law, and the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation at its meeting on 22 March 2017 adopted this draft law without amendments and on its behalf, but also on my own, I would like to ask the High Sejm to adopt it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u31	Thank you very much, sir. And on behalf of the Civic Platform Club, Mr Norbert Obrycki will perform. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u32	Thank you. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Civic Platform Club to present the position of the club vis-à-vis the government's draft law on investment in the construction or reconstruction of the Świnoujście watercourse - Szczecin to a depth of 12.5 meters. The contested bill is the best example of the fact that what builds the strength of our country is the continuation of multi-annual investment programmes, regardless of what the governing team is currently in power. The draft law, as indicated in the applicant's explanatory statement, refers to the third stage of the work, which is extremely difficult and expensive, but necessary for Odra to be not only navigable, but also attractive businessly due to the deepening of the Szczecin - Świnoujście watercourse throughout its length to a depth of 12.5 m. It should be recalled here that the first two stages have been successfully completed and are an essential starting point for further work. The first stage of the modernization of the watercourse in 2000 and 2004 included the construction of the starting waves from the Piast Canal to the Szczeciński Zalew and the reconstruction of the shores of the eastern and western sides of the canal on a section of 3 km of the length of the track. The second stage, carried out entirely under the rule of the Civic Platform and the Polish People's Party, took over the reconstruction of the coastal fortifications of the Piast Canal and the Małeński Canal on the eastern and western sides of the section with a length of more than 8 km of the watercourse. The modernised edge reinforcements have been adapted to the target track depth of 12.5 m. Construction works also included the removal of navigation obstacles, the landing of bays after the former Karsibor ferry crossing, the construction of technical quay on the island of Malin and the restoration of waterfront No 91. Under the Civic Platform, all the strategic documents needed for this ambitious task were also prepared: feasibility study for investment, environmental impact report, mathematical model of watercourse, complete navigation analysis of the upgraded track and many other supporting studies. This draft law anticipates the problems that may arise with the necessary expropriation of landowners in this case and the problems arising from local authorities' competence to make the necessary changes to the spatial planning plans. Its aim is primarily to introduce procedural facilitations related to this. The draft law also sets out in detail the scope of the water track modernisation investment and indicates the source of financing for the investment, i.e. Operational programme - Infrastructure and the environment for the period 2014-2020. This investment is undoubtedly strategic for the economy and state security and is essential for the development of ports in Szczecin and Świnoujście. It is therefore essential for the national economy and to ensure the safety of shipping on the watercourse. It is included in the implementation document for the 2020 transport development strategy with a view to 2030. For obvious reasons, the Civic Platform Club supports the proposed solutions. We are pleased that the rulers have now recognised the achievements of previous governing teams, including the Civic Platform, and continue their work on the modernisation and deepening of the Świnoujście watercourse - Szczecin to a depth of 12.5 m throughout its length, i.e. Sixty-seven miles. We hope that the process of ensuring the right conditions to implement this ambitious strategic action will proceed quickly and efficiently, respecting citizens' ownership rights and their right to fair compensation for expropriation from areas affected by the Act. The Civic Platform will continue to support all activities aimed at modernising the Szczecin-Świnoujście watercourse and improving access to the port in Szczecin. We will also observe how the investment process itself will proceed, in view of the enormous scope of work that will be carried out. The proposed special act introduces procedural facilitations, but in the field of pre-investment preparatory work. We hope that the implementation of the relevant investment will take place as smoothly as the present special act is being conducted. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u34	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Kukiz Poselski Club15 in relation to the report of the committee on the government draft law on investments in the construction or reconstruction of the Szczecin - Świnoujście watercourse to a depth of 12.5 meters. Deepening the Szczecin - Świnoujście watercourse to this depth will increase the availability of the Szczecin - Świnoujście port, which will facilitate the transport and export of goods, ensure the development of around-port services and allow the elimination of shipping restrictions, including. on designation of the so-called. It's a mess. The explanatory memorandum states that its purpose is to introduce procedural facilitations in pre-investment preparatory work. Solutions are envisaged to enable rapid preparation and smooth implementation of the deepening of the watercourse along with the necessary accompanying work. This will reduce the procedural stage by about 6 months, which hopefully will contribute to an efficient public spending. This is important because it is planned that over PLN 1385 million will have to be allocated to the investment. This welcomes the fact that this proposal proposes provisions to simplify the environmental procedure in order to confirm its compliance with the local spatial planning plan. Good. It is proposed to exempt the investor from the obligation to present extracts and drawings from the spatial planning plan when submitting an application for an opinion on the environmental impact assessment of the project and the whole Natura 2000 area, as well as when applying for a decision on environmental conditions. Kukiz.15 considers that legislation to facilitate the acquisition of land on which family gardens are located is a good solution. This will allow easier and faster acquisition of garden areas for investment, with the possibility of amicable settlement of disputes. We believe that such solutions are in our national interest. The draft law also sets out a procedural path in the event of exclusion from the board of State Land Forests, which according to the construction project will be used for investment. The deepening of the Szczecin - Świnoujście watercourse to a depth of 12.5 m is one of the basic investments, connected not only with the development of ports in Szczecin, but also an important impulse for the development of the entire region. The technical depth of 68 km of the water track connecting Świnoujście and Szczecin almost over the entire length is 10.5 m. This allows for safe shipping only ships with a draught of up to 9,15 m. The Baltic standard for seawater depth in seaports or container terminals is currently 15 and 16 m. The condition for maintaining competition through the port in Szczecin is the deepening of this track. The location of Szczecin inland can be an asset of the port. It offers a chance to reach by cheaper seaways much closer to the recipients and shippers of their cargo. A similar location in inland areas, which is closer to the facilities and economic centres located there, can be seen in many European ports. An example is the port in Hamburg - it is a 110 km long track from the open sea and also in Antwerp, where the distance from the sea to the port is 130 km. Most transhipments there indicate that these ports are developing and constitute important economic centres of the regions and countries in which they operate. If we want to join the European lead, we should, of course, deepen the watercourse to 12.5 m. This Act allows proper preparation for this investment. We as the Kukiz Posel Club15 will, of course, vote in favour of this law, in favour of these solutions. We are pleased that all parliamentary clubs in the Sejm will support this direction because it serves the whole community of Szczecin, Polic, Świnoujście. And this is really the flywheel of this part of Poland. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u36	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Modern Club, it is my honor to present the position of our club regarding this bill. As the Members and Members of this House know very well, one of the most important factors for economic growth is the development of international trade, and this exchange, which is still the most cost-effective, is carried out through maritime transport. Seaports in Szczecin and Świnoujście form one of the largest and most universal port complexes in the Baltic Sea. It is also one of the most important transport hubs in the South Baltic region. The most important advantage is its location on the shortest road connecting Scandinavia to central and southern Europe, and its location on the shortest sea road connecting, via the Baltic, Russia, Finland, Lithuania, Latvia and Estonia with Germany and beyond - Western Europe. However, at present, units that can be accommodated in the Szczecin port have a maximum immersion of up to 9 m, and in practice even smaller, resulting in a de facto marginalisation of the position of the Szczecin port in a competitive market for southern Baltic port services. Many customers are forced to select alternative ports, i.e. e.g. in Rostock, which has a immersion of up to 13 m, or in Hamburg - up to 14,4 m, which translates, of course, into a smaller quantity of goods carried in Poland. The adoption of the Act, not so much its adoption as its real implementation, is of key strategic importance primarily for Szczecin, the West Pomeranian voivodeship, but also for the whole Republic, creating the basis for the implementation of investments included in the 2020 Transport Development Strategy, in particular by providing for the effective implementation of further infrastructure and industrial projects, namely: maintaining the conditions for the operation and expansion of the external port in Świnoujście by creating a position for the export of LNG, developing port infrastructure in the southern part of the port of Świnoujście to enable mooring and handling of ro-pax ferries up to 220 m long with accompanying infrastructure, adapting the infrastructure of the Świnoujście Ferry Terminal to intermodal transport, developing port infrastructure in the port in Szczecin by improving the availability of the quay with improved navigation conditions in the Dębicki Canal and the Kaszubsko Basin Basin, expanding the port in the Poles with a terminal for liquid chemicals in relation to the investment of Police 2 - the most modern and largest installations in Europe for the production of propylene PDH method. This action is consistent with the priorities of the European Union, which places great emphasis on improving transport corridors via environmentally friendly modes of transport. The investment will ensure an increase in freight mass, which will pass through the Baltic - Adriatic corridor through ports in Świnoujście and Szczecin. The idea of creating motorways of the sea is linked to the need to develop alternative transport connections for intermodal transport. From the organizational and investment side, both the Board of Maritime Ports Szczecin and Świnoujście, as well as the Swedish partners of the port, as well as the ship owners operating on this line, are prepared to handle additional cargo mass. The Act, supporting economic functions in the transport, logistics and industrial field, provides conditions for port development by contributing to the TEN-T trans-European transport network programme co-financed by the European Investment Bank, the European Structural and Investment Funds and the Connecting Europe Facility for which the aim is to improve the availability of seaports and coastal infrastructure while supporting maritime transport as alternatives to other modes of transport. Ladies and gentlemen! Deepening the watercourse of Szczecin - Świnoujście is our duty, which we cannot abandon regardless of who is in charge of the ship of Rzeczpospolita. Captain Kaczyński, you lead our ship very often to the shallow, but in the case of this Act, the Modern will vote in favour of it. I hope that this position will be voted unanimously by the High House. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u98	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the report of the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs in print No. 1398 on the draft law amending the Act on the Social Security System, print No. 1256, and on the governmental bill amending the Act on the Social Insurance System and the Act amending the Labour Code Act and some other laws, print No. 1306. The print project No 1256 was submitted to the Sejm by Members of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on 20 January 2017 and its main objective is to abolish the instrument of compulsory punishment for the institution serving the payment of contributions and contributions who made a mistake in the documents transferred to the Social Insurance Institution. Currently from the content of the article. For the purposes of this Regulation, the following definitions shall apply: 10c of the Social Insurance Act shows that an additional fee is made compulsory by the Social Security Office when two conditions are met in total: firstly, the Social Security Office did not transfer to OFE a contribution to the insured person within the statutory deadline, secondly, the fault for the failure of the Social Security Office is on the payer of the contributions or the institution serving the payment of the contributions, which results from the failure to provide or incorrect transfer to the Social Insurance Institution by those entities to the Social Insurance Institution, the declaration of the payer of the contributions, the monthly registered report, the billing declaration, the payment document or the payment order. Thus, this applies when mistakes have been made, not to non-payment. An additional fee shall not be charged if it does not exceed PLN 2. This is again the case in the present state, because there was also a situation that the limit amount from which no fee is charged was PLN 6.60. This project was generally approved, although the opinion of the Chamber of Commerce of pension societies also appeared. In my view, this is due to a misunderstanding of the proposed solutions, as the House in its opinion even stresses that the implementation of the proposed changes could lead to the abuse by employers of the resulting legal gap by avoiding the payment of contributions due to OFE to the Social Security Office. I would like to emphasise once again, as I have already said, that this change, the removal of this additional fee, concerns an additional charge imposed by obligation for mistakes made. This does not apply to the additional fee referred to in previous provisions, when the Social Security Office may grant the payer an additional fee of up to 100% unpaid contributions. So when the payer doesn't pay, the ZUS still has the option to charge an additional fee. This opinion also stated that the explanatory memorandum provided only an average additional fee of one year, from 2014, but also that this average fee could be PLN 14 and here the Chamber claims that otherwise it could be up to 20,000. zł. The average additional fee, of course, was in the justification of this project given from 2014, but from the opinions prepared by the Sejm Analysis Office, e.g. of the opinion of 9 December for the period 1999 to 2010 for all these years, the average fee of one decision was PLN 15.54 and for 2015 the fee was PLN 13.59. So she absolutely didn't influence that benefit, that fee transferred to OFE would have any effect on the insured person. The project was referred to first reading in the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs, which started working on it at its meeting on 22 February. The government bill amending the Social Security Act and the Act amending the Labour Code Act and some other laws from print No. 1306 entered the Sejm on 14 February this year. The first reading took place at the Sejm meeting on 8 March, and then the Sejm sent the draft to the committee. The Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy at its meeting on 22 March 2017 decided to consider both projects jointly and as a leader we adopted the government project. The primary objective of the government proposal for a law amending the Act on the Social Insurance System and the Act amending the Labour Code Act and some other laws is to introduce a uniform standard of identification of the payer of social security contributions, health insurance, the Labour Fund and the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund and the Pension Fund. The project assumes the introduction of individual contribution accounts for contributors to which a single payment for all the charges to which the Social Insurance Institution is obliged to collect will be made. In the current legal situation, pursuant to Article. For the purposes of this Regulation, the following definitions shall apply: 4 of the Act of 13 October 1998 on the social security system, the contributors are obliged to pay contributions to bank accounts with separate payments broken down into social security, health insurance, the Labour Fund and the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund and the Pension Fund. So the payer is obliged to make up to four monthly payments. Most often, contributions to the Pomeranian Pension Fund relate not to such a large number of payers, but with regard to the remaining three payments, thousands of payers have to make three transfers each month, pay three transfers, and unfortunately there is a threefold higher risk of making mistakes. Although this has been in place since 1999, it has not even undergone major changes, and because of the adoption of such a solution in the 1998 Act, this raises many problems. Payments often make mistakes, precisely those errors that have been punished with additional charges. Therefore, in our opinion, in the opinion of the committee, this solution will make the life of the payers much simpler, in colloquially speaking. The draft law requires the establishment to generate by 31 December 2017 the number of individual contribution accounts for payers of contributions entered in the Central Register of Payments for Contributions at the date of entry into force of the Act within a time limit allowing them to be effectively informed and made available to those accounts the payment of contributions for January 2018. The proposed solution will fundamentally change the way in which payment accounts are cleared and will be widely used, e.g. also in the case of short-term benefits rights for insured persons who are subject to voluntary benefits. To put it simply, when it comes to payers who pay their own contributions, i.e. business people, it is voluntary to join sickness insurance and they must pay their contributions in time to be subject to this insurance. And so far, the question of how to describe the payment has often affected whether the insured person was entitled to this benefit or not. Today - in my opinion, I think that also the committee which is proposing this proposal to the High Sejm, as will be concluded, acceptable - will greatly simplify the situation and the payers, and serve the payers by the Social Security Office. The draft also clarifies certain provisions of the Social Insurance Act and arranges these provisions. In addition, the proposal proposes, as in this parliamentary case, the repeal of Article. For the purposes of this Regulation, the following definitions shall apply: 10c-10g, i.e. repealing the rules on the levying of an additional fee, and giving a new wording to the paragraph. 10h and 10i. Each of these groups of contributions was paid into separate bank accounts, which meant that three and in some cases even four transfers should have been made each month. The project introduces individual premium accounts for which one payment will be made for all the charges to which the Social Insurance Institution is obliged to collect. The number of the new account will be assigned by the Social Insurance Institution and will become the new identifier of the payer of the contributions. The project provides that the undertaking will be responsible for the distribution of funds between individual funds and their managers. Changes will reduce the amount of information provided on payment documents, thus reducing errors made by payers and improving the settlement system with the Social Security Office. The number of payments to be processed by banks' IT systems and the Social Security Office will also decrease annually from 65 million to around 27 million, so this is a significant decrease. The project also addresses the introduction of facilitations for contributors when it comes to the issue of certificates of non-payment by the Social Security Office. This will be possible through legal alignment of paper certificates and electronic printouts produced by the payer from ZUS electronic systems. In addition, the payer and the insured will have faster access to information about the balance of the account, and the clearing system will be simplified using modern financial solutions. It is justified to adopt a uniform model of compensation for late payment of contributions to OFE in the form of interest paid by the Social Security Office without detailed analysis of whose fault this delay occurred. In addition, the purpose of this draft law is to clarify certain provisions so as to eliminate differences in interpretation. Given that the proposed amendments aim to increase the efficiency and effectiveness of the Social Insurance Institution, the Law and Justice Club supports this project and will vote in favour of it. However, we are proposing an amendment on the basis of Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, to the Act amending the Act on the Social Security System and the Act amending the Labour Code Act and some other laws. This amendment is based on the fact that Article. 1 point (10) (a) g is replaced by the following: If the contribution has not been transferred to the open pension fund within the deadline, interest due shall be calculated from the establishment at the variable interest rate applicable for the subsequent three-month periods beginning on the first day of each calendar quarter. The variable interest rate shall be calculated as the arithmetic average of the reference rates of the National Bank of Poland in force during the period of 3 months until the end of the month preceding the last month before the beginning of the quarter concerned and shall be rounded to two decimal places. For the calculation of interest, the year is assumed to be 365 days. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u125	Thank you very much. I will ask the Undersecretary of State in the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy of Mr Marcin Zielenecki for answers to the questions asked. You're welcome, Minister, to respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u126	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Today's debate is focused on two draft laws amending the Social Security Act: firstly, a parliamentary proposal on the issue of charging additional fees in the event of mispayment or non-payment of social security contributions and, secondly, a government project relating to a much wider spectrum of issues. I think that the course of the discussion shows that the issues, the solutions proposed in both drafts of the amendment of the law are not controversial. I would like to thank all the representatives of all the parliamentary clubs for their clear support for both draft laws. By the way, I would like to thank Mrs Barbara Bartus very warmly, [[Applause]] who made a huge effort to prepare the parliamentary bill, as well as the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, which submitted the bill to the Sejm. As I said, the convergence of the proposals contained in the government project and in the parliamentary project led us to consider both projects together. In general, the subject of the government project is, one can say, the introduction of a new standard of services provided by the Social Insurance Institution: the standard that we know and which is already used for other types of payments - payments made in the private sector - a standard to which we are actually all accustomed. I mean the method of making payments for electricity or gas charges. We are used to the fact that each person making such a payment has an individual bank account number assigned to it. We all, I think, see the benefits of this solution. It can be said that the government project envisages the introduction of the same modern standard of services provided by the Social Insurance Institution for nearly 7 million contributors. This is one of the directions of the proposed solutions. The second is to simplify and re-bureaucratise procedures for the payment of social security contributions. The discussion raised the question of how the bill would make life easier for contributors, including entrepreneurs, employers. As I said, this is a project relating to 7 million contributors. In place of the existing four separate transfers, only one transfer will be carried out by the payer, while the allocation of funds to individual funds will be carried out by the payer, i.e. The Social Insurance Fund, the National Health Fund, together with the Guaranteed Occupational Benefits Fund and the Labour Fund, as well as the Bridge Pension Fund as the fourth of these transfers, the burden of calculating these funds and dividing them between all funds will be borne by the Social Insurance Institution. It can be said that the number of transfers and, therefore, the costs associated with these transfers will also be reduced four times, so it is undoubtedly a simplification, a rebureaucratisation, but also a reduction in the costs associated with the payment of social security contributions. At the same time, I would like to say that I do not share those concerns which were formulated during the discussion on the draft law, concerns concerning, above all, decisions relating to the establishment of contributions by the Social Insurance Institution. I would like to say that the Social Insurance Institution already has competence in determining the payer's contributions. Such decisions, determining who is subject to a premium obligation, are simply issued on the occasion of other decisions taken by the Social Insurance Institution, i.e. the decision to determine the social security obligation or the decision to set a premium obligation. In the context of the procedure for issuing such decisions, the Social Insurance Institution shall also determine who is subject to the premium obligation. As regards the role of the proposed provisions in the definition of the payer of contributions or the new Article. 38a of the law or a new provision in Article. 83 of the Act, the role of these provisions is in fact merely to clarify and unambiguously indicated that, in the event of doubt about who is the payer of the contributions, the Social Insurance Institution will be able to determine by decision who the obligation to pay the contributions rests on. With regard to the reason for introducing this provision, it is directly linked to the phenomenon of outsourcing of workers and - it can be said - with certain doubts, problems relating to the consequences of decisions by the labour courts to determine the apparent legal act of transferring workers to the new employer under Article. The 23rd Labour Code. The second concern that was raised during the discussion concerned the principle that the charges or contributions made by the payers would be credited to the oldest unpaid contributions. Of course, we are talking about a situation in which there are arrears, so this is a solution that - we have to say - aims to discipline the contributors to ensure that they carry out their contribution obligations in a timely and correct manner. We do not see the negative consequences of the introduction of this principle, in terms of - it can be said - the right to social security benefits. This is a solution, I would say, quite natural in a situation where, through the IT system and using the individual account of the payer, the contribution obligation will be implemented. As regards the remaining questions that were raised in the course of the discussion, the question was raised concerning the introduction of a threshold of PLN 2 for the decision to charge an additional fee. So in a government project, we generally give up the possibility of making such decisions at all. The provisions that provide for the possibility of imposing such an additional fee on the payer of the contributions, we simply propose to delete them. As regards the question of Mrs Barbara Bartus concerning the limitation period of claims and the different timing of the start of this 5-year limitation period, this is an interesting proposal. I can promise you we'll take a closer look at her and take a formal approach to her. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u195	Mr. Marshal! Dear Minister, High Izbo! I wouldn't want to... Waldemar Buda. I would not like to repeat the principles of the bill, because we discussed them at first reading and during the work in committee, but I would like to refer to this current stage, namely the report of the deregulation committee and its content. The fact is that during the work in committee there was a change in the draft with respect to its original version, namely that the record of the pharmacist was removed as the only entity authorised to run pharmacies. It was a kind of compromise which, leaving the remaining possibilities, the remaining mechanisms of regulation resulting from this law, gave little but some possibilities to regulate, civilise this market. Unfortunately, the state has alleged here that we ignore opinions, such as the legal opinions that appear in this case, but it is exactly the opposite. It was issued that BAS issued its legal opinion, which was the result of which - opinion 357/17 of 23 March 2017. - that the act in the form of the report is incompatible with the Constitution and European law, i.e. it entails restrictions on economic activity which are not justified by the specific objective of the patient's well-being. Indeed, this is because if we remove the pharmaceutical provision, there is no justification to limit the running of the pharmacy to capital companies. If a natural person, e.g. A mechanic would be able to run a pharmacy, so why couldn't capital in the form of a capital company do it as well? It is therefore necessary to return to the original version of the project and this is what these amendments concern. Firstly, restoring the role of pharmacist as the only person authorised to run pharmacies. Secondly, maintaining the restriction on the exclusion of capital companies from the possibility of pharmacies. Thirdly, a few minor legislative changes that improve this project. There is another amendment that responds to your demands. That is to say, you have pointed out many examples that there may be a situation in which termination of rental contracts or the need to change the location of the pharmacy may result in a new location, even very close to it, a permit being impossible due to geographical and demographic conditions. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u250	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! I was honoured to present the report of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs and the Committee on Foreign Affairs on the Government's draft Act on the ratification of the Agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Government of the Republic of Kazakhstan on the readmission of persons signed in Warsaw on 22 August 2016 in print No. 1348. Marshal of the Sejm, according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, after consulting the Bureau of the Sejm, sent on 8 March 2017 a draft law to the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs and the Committee on Foreign Affairs for first reading. The reasons for binding the Republic of Poland to this agreement on the readmission of persons under ratification with the prior consent of the Act are the following conditions. Primo, the agreement concerns the matters referred to in Article. Article 89 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 point 5 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, i.e. matters governed by the law relating to the provision of officials of the Republic of Kazakhstan escorting a person of the same level of protection and support as the internal law guarantees to Polish officers. It's an art. Point 15 (6) of the Agreement. These issues are regulated, among others. By law of 6 June 1997, the Penal Code on the specific protection of a public officer in the performance of his duties, as well as the Border Guard Act, which imposes an obligation on governmental authorities, local government units and other state organisational units to cooperate with Border Guard authorities and to provide them with the necessary conditions for the performance of their statutory tasks, including assistance in the field of applicable law. Secundo, the application by officials of the Republic of Kazakhstan on the territory of the Republic of Poland of measures of direct coercion against a person subject to transfer or transit under escort constitutes a restriction on the freedom guaranteed by that person and in accordance with Article. Article 41 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland may take place only in accordance with the rules and procedures laid down in the Act. In view of the above, following the fulfilment of the conditions laid down in Article. Article 89 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 points 2 and 5 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, the binding of the Republic of Poland by this agreement should take place by ratification with the prior consent expressed in the Act, in accordance with Article. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of 14 April 2000 on international agreements. A joint meeting of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs and the Committee on Foreign Affairs was held on 22 March. After a detailed presentation by the Secretary of State in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs of Mr Jakub Skiba of the bill on ratification of the bilateral agreement and more than 20 years' history of work prior to the signing of the agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Government of the Republic of Kazakhstan on readmission of persons of 22 August 2016, and after discussing its basic content, Members did not submit any comments or doubts. Nor did they take advantage of the possibility of asking questions. The representative of the Legislative Office has not submitted comments on the project. The chairman of the session, Mr Robert Tyszkiewicz, called for a vote. 23 Members voted in favour of the committee's report requesting the adoption of Draft Print Act No. 1348, no one was against and no one abstained. Therefore, on behalf of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs and the Committee on Foreign Affairs, after first reading and considering the draft law, I ask that the High Sejm adopt without amendment the law in the wording proposed by the Government in print No 1348, as expressed by the committees in the report in print No 1416. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u276	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a government bill on farmers' cooperatives, in which we refer to the best traditions of Polish cooperatives. Cooperatives in Poland during the interwar period were in many cases a model for the whole of Europe. Cooperatives in the countryside have developed since the partitions, because already then farmers believed that the survival of Polish agriculture during this difficult period requires joint action. In the interwar period, farmers' cooperatives operated in many areas of agricultural support, in many areas of joint institution building, which allowed the scale of joint action to generate additional benefits to make Polish agriculture stronger. In the post-war period, the idea of cooperatives was distorted because cooperatives were included in the social economy units, ceased to be private property of members of cooperatives and thus cooperatives became part of the economy of the then totalitarian state. After 1989, cooperatives functioned in a very diverse way, but most of the previous cooperatives either fell, or significantly reduced their activities, failing to withstand competition with the predatory market, which then appeared in Poland, without holding back competition with private capital, without holding back competition with foreign capital. Over the years, the Polish State has not been able and has not offered farmers the opportunity to organise themselves in cooperatives in such a way that, on the one hand, they can actually achieve added value, additional effects on the scale of their activities and, on the other hand, so that they have any, albeit minor, preferences. In the proposal presented by the government to the High House, we are returning to the best traditions, but we are also proposing solutions that allow for additional support, additional assistance, especially in the early years of the cooperative's operation. When we look at other countries of the European Union, where the degree of organisation of farmers is much higher than in Poland, we see how much has been lost in Poland, how much has to be done in Poland. In the Netherlands, Ireland, France, Germany, the degree of organisation of agricultural producers in farmers' cooperatives ranges from 70% to 98%. In Poland it is currently statistically 15% , but looking at the structure of Polish cooperatives, this organization is even smaller in the effects of cooperative activities. The milk market in Denmark is 94% share of the cooperative sector, the pig market in France is 94% , the cereal market in France is 74% , sugar market in France, Netherlands, Spain, Italy - approx. 40% , the fruit and vegetables market in the Netherlands, Belgium, Sweden is 70%. Among the solutions that we propose in this law, the most important are the comprehensive regulation of the rules and functioning of agricultural cooperatives. Of course, agricultural cooperatives will still be subject to the fundamental cooperative right, which applies to all other cooperatives. But farmers' cooperatives, which should aim at doing business for their members in terms of production planning, adjusting the scale and quality of production to market expectations, concentration of supply, organisation of the sale of agricultural products to their members and concentration of demand and organisation of the purchase of agricultural inputs, will have additional preferences, additional regulations resulting from the proposed High House for the adoption of a law. The cooperative of farmers will be able to set up at least 10 farmers. The incentives put in place in this project are incentives in the form of an exemption from inheritance tax and donations, acquired contributions rights in farmers' cooperatives, exemptions from tax and local charges on buildings and buildings and parts thereof, as well as land seized by the cooperative or the association of farmers' cooperatives for the activities set out in the Act, i.e. those activities which involve the organisation of farmers in order to improve their situation, and corporation tax where the exemption will concern the income of farmers' cooperatives from the sale of agricultural products or groups of those products for which the cooperative was established. It will be a condition to produce these products on the agricultural holdings of the members of the cooperative. These reliefs are primarily intended to help cooperatives in the early stages of their activities, so they will concern those cooperatives which employ no more than 10 employees and whose... less than 10 employees and whose annual turnover or balance sheet total does not exceed EUR 2 million. Such a solution aims to create preferential conditions for newly created entities so that they can fulfil their tasks. The cooperative of farmers will create own funds: equity, stock and reciprocity. The draft also includes adaptation provisions that adapt the provisions of other laws to ensure that the project can function and be consistent with other acts. I hope that this project will help to increase the organisation of agricultural producers in Poland, to strengthen the process of creating small and medium-sized enterprises in the agricultural environment, in agriculture, to increase the economic strength of farms, to improve competitiveness in the agricultural product market, but also to raise awareness of the importance of organising farmers in common structures. As regards support for economic development, we predict that cooperatives will be able to report the need for means of production in a relatively larger quantity than single producers did and thus to obtain better prices. The project requires notification due to the exemption establishing public aid and the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development made such notification on 20 March this year. The project was consulted in the context of a public consultation and was addressed to 108 organisations. Nine organisations responded, seven made comments. The vast majority of the comments were taken into account, while for the others the reasons why the project could not be revised were broadly explained. High Izbo! On behalf of the Government of the Republic of Poland, I request that work be undertaken on the bill.

It gives real benefits to the operation of the cooperative model, the pursuit of a common economic activity, which is intended to strengthen the market position of peasant farms - precisely through this cooperative model. The exemption from real estate and corporate income tax is an important support for the organisation and operation of cooperatives in this form. Is this enough encouragement? I do not know, no one is able to determine whether this is enough to make cooperatives an important organizer of economic life in rural areas in Polish agriculture. We are generally individualists and it is difficult for us to cooperate with anyone, but it seems that the new generations that are entering agriculture no longer have this mental burden with a negative assessment of communist cooperatives, and maybe with more confidence than even my generation or my parents will want to create, carry out cooperative activities. Therefore - without getting into the details, because this law should, of course, be addressed to the committee; we need to work, to consider whether any other form of support could be applied - it is a law that goes absolutely in the right direction. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u284	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I have the honour to present my position on the government's draft law on farmers' cooperatives. Cooperatives, as a form of society's association, have many advantages, the most important of which are voluntaryity, initiative capacity, cooperation and mutual assistance in the event of emerging threats. There is no doubt that by combining knowledge, skills, experience and the involvement of many people together, we are able to achieve more than one person. It is important for members of cooperatives to be transparent, democratic voting rules, the inviolable right to decide how to manage their assets, and therefore the future law should protect and ensure the best interests of farmers associated with cooperatives. All over the world, cooperatives are the main form of citizens' association, which has a significant share in many sectors of the economy. For example, in the Netherlands almost 100% farmers are associated in cooperatives and more than 90% of all dairy and pig processing establishments are cooperatives. In France, farmers control more than half of the agricultural processing industry through cooperatives, and agricultural cooperatives - and their associations - are employers for around 150 000. people, which significantly contributes to the reduction of unemployment in rural areas. In Poland, agricultural cooperatives are not as popular as in Western European countries, which I hope will change this law. I do not want to focus on the analysis of the causes of Poles' dislike for cooperatives, I would like to draw attention to what I think is most important for the functioning of cooperatives, namely mutual trust between the people who form the cooperative and who are members of it. Trust is the basis on which each cooperative should be founded - it ensures stability and security that the cooperative's activities will not be lost by the irresponsible activities of the individual. The cooperative operates an activity which cannot be threatened by the inclusion of random persons in a series of cooperatives. Therefore, I cannot agree with the proposal contained in the proposal that the cooperative of farmers should not have the right to refuse to accept members of a farmer or non-farmer if it meets the requirements set out in the Act. It is understandable that the provisions of the Statutes should lay down the terms and conditions that a farmer wishing to join a cooperative must fulfil, but because of the fact that, at the outset, I have clearly stressed the essence of mutual trust in the functioning of the cooperative, entrusting the issue of membership only with rigidly defined provisions of the Statutes poses a serious threat to the existence of a cooperative and will discourage this form of association. A human factor that cannot be closed into a formal framework, and which is manifested, among others, in the form of a formal framework. in responsibility, maturity, degree of commitment, is the most important and cooperative members should decide who will be a member of it. Please note that cooperatives often have large assets which are owned by cooperative members, and that none of the States would agree that the right to co-decision on the property which you have worked on for many years would be affected by a random person who became a cooperative member because he meets the requirements set out in the Act. In conclusion, the solutions presented in the Government's draft law on farmers' cooperatives are heading in the right direction, and in the light of the doubts indicated, I propose that the draft law be sent to the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development in order to discuss the whole in detail and to introduce the necessary amendments. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u318	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I answer your questions. As regards support for cooperatives under the measures at the disposal of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, all these mechanisms will be available which are available to such legal entities already or for which recruitments will be carried out. There is no separate form of support, there is no separate path of support for this structure, but of course these entities will be able to benefit from any support that is available to legal entities, to cooperatives active in agriculture. When it comes to actions concerning existing cooperatives that have survived - this question has been repeated twice - it must be borne in mind that there is such an old principle: the absence of a reward is not a penalty. The lack of additional regulation is not a penalty for existing cooperatives, nothing is worse for these cooperatives, nothing is taken away. The support mechanism provided for in the case of the creation of cooperatives is a mechanism that operates at the beginning of the functioning of the new entity. It must be borne in mind that the income tax relief not only deprives the State of its income but also reduces the revenue of local governments, that the real estate tax exemption is entirely borne by the local government. That is why we have decided on a solution that will not be charged to local governments. It seems impossible, but yes, because we assume that there will be entities that, if not for this bill, would never have been created, which would never have been. So they don't have those payers, they don't exist at the moment. There will be, farmers will join in cooperatives, but in this way they will not reduce the income potential of local governments, and at the most there will be no effect in terms of income increases. I think this is a compromise that allows us to encourage farmers to join, but at the same time not to burden them with reducing their income potential. The number of members. Why so big? It seems to me that it is a little too small to be a strong economic entity, given the structure of our agriculture and how big cooperatives are, which are doing well on European markets, also on the Polish market. In general, they are members of at least a dozen farmers. [[Thousands even.]] Maybe hundreds, thousands less. So 10 is not a very large number, but it is also important to remember that we have bad experiences with these small players, in the case of producer groups, and five members were admitted as founders of the group. Unfortunately, too often it happened that the founders of the group were five members, including four related family members, from a holding which had previously been divided, and the group was formed only to obtain grants and not to achieve any added effect. [[It's deviant.]] So the number 10 is, it seems, a minimum number and an optimal number. This is also the answer to the question of why we have not decided to prevent farmers who are interested from joining the cooperative. We want them to be open players, so that they are actors to which farmers will come together and be able to join. Of course, if anyone is harming the cooperative, they should be excluded, and the relevant statutory body of the cooperative has the right to exclude such a person. As far as income tax is concerned, it does only apply to the part that comes from the sale of raw materials, agricultural products produced on the holding concerned. As far as property tax is concerned, the Member is right that a processing plant can also be set up, but let us remember that we have a limitation: 2 million balance sheet total or annual turnover. This is not the amount that allows for the creation of a large company, that is the amount that allows the creation of a possible small factory. At this stage, support should be provided and we are determined to provide such support to the full extent as is apparent from the Article. Article 6 (6) (a) 1 and 2, the object of the operation of such a cooperative. One more basic question. It's a cooperative case, it's a group thing. There's no contradiction here. If a cooperative of farmers is set up under this law, it may at the same time meet the criteria required to be a producer group and benefit from the support which is addressed to producer groups in the current financial perspective. And finally, what tools do we use to encourage farmers to join the cooperative? First of all, good examples need to be shown. Yesterday, I had a group of farmers who had created not a cooperative, because it was the previous cooperative law that they considered too unattractive for themselves, but the association of producers. These farmers were able to accumulate in excess of 100, they could sell jointly bred animals, they could also collect several million zlotys in their organization's account to support themselves later in order to develop their activities. Such good examples of cooperatives were created in accordance with this law. They will be the best incentive for farmers to unite, because it is worth integrating their activities, it is worth joining cooperatives. I would like to thank you for the substantive discussion on this project and I would like to thank you for your willingness to continue working in committee. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u322	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a government bill amending the Act on National Judicial School and Public Prosecutor's Office, the Law on the System of General Courts and some other laws, print No 1406. Maybe I should talk first about the reasons for the amendment. By Act of 18 March 2016 on the amendment of the Law on the System of General Courts, the Act on the National School of Judicial Affairs and the Public Prosecutor's Office and certain other laws of the Republic of Poland, the formation of the first assessors positions in the general court was postponed until 30 October 2017 and the selection of new, partially decentralised specialized applications was postponed over a year, thus extending the functioning of the existing two-step model of initial education covering the general application and the following specialized applications, i.e. judicial and prosecutorial applications. The legislator pointed out that the training model at the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office adopted in 2015 had disadvantages that had to be eliminated before its implementation. The analysis of the functioning of the national school since its establishment in 2009 and the existing models of coming to the profession of the judge in Poland indicates that there are no statutory guarantees for graduates of the national school who receive professional training, the receipt of judicial or asessor posts despite the passed judicial examination. This is an obvious social loss and a loss of high financial expenditure incurred annually by the state budget for education at the national school. Only 28 referee applicants from 165 referee applicants who have the power to apply for vacant judicial positions have been appointed. This is the best proof of this thesis I've just presented. The analysis I am talking about has also shown the existence in practice of the functioning of the general judicial system shortcomings and bureaucratic regulation which are unnecessary in terms of efficiency in the management of justice. The draft amendment provides for the regulation of the legal situation of persons who have completed their judicial application at the national school and those who are currently taking part in it or who will take place under existing rules by 2020. During this time, applicants who started training on the general application on 30 January 2017 will take the judge's exam. Graduates and applicants of the first four years of the judge's application, in accordance with the current legal state, are guaranteed the right to apply by means of a competition for the position of judge of the regional court. This power shall be exercised by virtue of Article. 24 of the Law of 10 July 2015 amending the Law on the System of General Courts and certain other laws would expire on 31 December 2022. Next year's graduates from V to VIII are obliged to go through the judicial assesura, and they must apply for the position of asessor in competitions, competing with lawyers representing other professions, e.g. with lawyers. Therefore, we are calling for the following changes in this law. We propose that a new model be introduced to the profession of judge. The judicial application carried out at the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office will constitute a basic way of reaching the profession of a judge with complementary recruitment of candidates for the judicial service from other legal professions. The amendments introduced by the law will contribute to the maximum number of applicants for judicial employment in the general court. It is reasonable for the institution of the judicial assessor to link to the adopted model of initial training of candidates for judicial positions. In the current state of law, access to the position of asessor was provided for many groups of candidates whose vocational training did not take place at such a substantive level and with as high public involvement as for graduates of the national school. The proposed regulation assumes that only those who have completed the application at the national school and have submitted a judge's or prosecutor's examination with positive results can be appointed as a judicial assessor. Assessor positions will be given priority to judicial applicants, however, in the absence of all assessor positions by the applicants of the judicial application in the criminal departments, the Minister of Justice will be able to order the appointment of these posts for prosecutors' applications. The head of the national school will publish a list of vacant posts and call on prosecutors who passed the prosecutor's exam in a given year to report their intention to take the position within 14 days. In view of the above, it is desirable that, after the application has been completed, the result of the judge's examination, possibly the prosecutor's decision on the priority of the admission to the judicial asseurium, and after the course of the service, if there is no objection to the course of the service, the assessor will receive a proposal for a judge's nomination without having to participate in the competition. The project assumes that the possibility of appointment as an assistant will also be extended to applicants of these yearbooks of the judge's application, who have already passed the judge's exam on the date of entry into force of this Act - we are talking here about the first four yearbooks of applicants - as well as the referees and assistants of judges who passed the national judge's exam in 2011/2016. If they do not choose an assessor position, they will have the opportunity to apply for a judicial position within 5 years from the date of entry into force of the Act. According to the draft, the Minister of Justice will appoint as a judicial assistant for an indefinite period. If the National Judicial Board does not object to the appointed assailant, it will be able to act as judge for a period of 4 years. In this way, the independence of the judicial assailant will be guaranteed, on the one hand, by breaking up after the act of appointment of the link between the assailant and the Minister of Justice and, on the other hand, by ensuring the influence of the National Judicial Council on the delegation of judicial assesors to serve as judges, as well as on the impact on further career as a judge. It restores the national school's full competence to organize applications, organise practices and restores the applicant's status as a national school scholar. There is a departure from the application for judicial and prosecutorial status of an employee of the court or of the prosecutor's office. It is therefore necessary to restore the rules on suspension of the applicant in his rights and obligations, as well as the rules on granting and reimbursement of the scholarship. The bill assumes that applicants will be covered by a scholarship system. Under the proposed regulations, applicants would not be protected under the universal social security system. The main feature of the proposed changes is the material support of applicants by paying a percentage of the scholarship, including in the case of illness and motherhood. The introduction of a mechanism to support applicants who become parents is necessary, among others. because of the need to implement the country's pro-family policy. The project also strengthens the position of the head of the national school and shapes the interaction of the national school authorities. There is a reduction in the composition of the Programme Board, as well as a reduction in its competences, as it becomes an advisory and advisory body in relation to the school head. According to the project, the Minister of Justice includes a national school of organisational supervision. The project involves a joint call for a judge and prosecutor's application. The proposed law grants the staff of the courts and the prosecutor's office an allowance for posting to the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office. In the current state of law, such an allowance shall be received only by an official of the court or the prosecutor's office delegated to the Ministry of Justice or the Public Prosecutor's Office. I will only present the most important amendments to the Law on the System of General Courts. Firstly, it is desirable to limit the possibility for judges to apply for a transfer to another place of business, which should ensure the stabilisation of judicial staff in individual courts. Secondly, it is proposed to introduce a regulation which will allow the disciplinary liability of the judge to be waived if he agrees to be held liable for the offence. These are, as a rule, traffic offences. Thirdly, it is assumed that there are grounds for granting leave to the judge by the Minister of Justice for the treatment of ill-health. Fourthly, in view of the need to eliminate the doubts arising from the existing content of the regulation, it is clear that the reimbursement of travel expenses is also payable to the judge when he carries out business duties outside the seat of the court, department or local centre. Analogical regulation exists in relation to prosecutors when they perform their duties at the local office of the prosecution's office. During absence from work due to illness, but no more than one year, the judge will receive 80% remuneration, but due to delays in the process before the National Council of the Court of Justice of applications for the transfer of the judge to rest after the end of this year, until the examination of the application, it is envisaged that the judges will be paid 75% salaries. The aim of this action is to provide adequate financial security for the period between the remainder of the judge's business relationship and his retirement. Exactly the same regulation is designed for prosecutors. This regulation is modelled on provisions concerning staff of state offices, judicial staff and prosecutors, assistants of judges, curators, referees, teachers or members of the civil service corps.
