ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u325	Thank you very much. Mr Arkadiusz Myrch will now speak on behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u326	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform would like to present its position on the draft law amending the Act on the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office, the Law on the System of General Courts and some other laws. The proposed reform significantly changes the functioning of the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office. However, these changes should be assessed critically. They lead to an increase in the degree of dependence on the Minister of Justice, not only the National Judicial School and the Public Prosecutor's Office, but also its graduates, i.e. future judges. These changes, which I stress with all determination and full responsibility, are dangerous for the independence and independence of the Polish justice system. They are in direct violation of the Constitution and are in contradiction with the case law of the Constitutional Court. It can be said that the projector did this intentionally to achieve a specific political objective, i.e. to ensure that the Minister of Justice has a real impact on the persons issuing judgments on behalf of the Republic of Poland. The draft law regulates the institution of the judicial assessor restored on 1 January 2016 after several years of interruption. However, the manner of appointment and dismissal described, as well as the scope of the powers conferred, raises huge concerns about practically all legal backgrounds. According to the existing rules, the judicial assessor as the person empowered to settle cases in court and deliver judgments should be appointed as judges, i.e. by the President at the request of the National Judicial Council following an objective competition. However, Minister Ziobro proposes that it should be for him, on a one-man basis, to appoint the assistants and delegate them to the duties of judge. This solution is clearly in breach of Article. 10 and Article 10 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers (OJ L 55, 28.2.2011, p. 1). 176 constitutions constituting a threefold division of power and separation of the judicial authority from the Such dependence on the Minister of Justice of the Tribunal has been found by the Constitutional Tribunal in its judgment of 24 October 2007 to be incompatible with the Constitution because of an infringement of the Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 providing that every citizen has the right to a fair trial. Minister Ziobro and his deputies are certainly familiar with the content of this ruling, but this solution is being introduced again with premeditated intent. Another proposal contained in the draft law, namely the preference of court assessors in the pursuit of the profession of judge, is not part of this. Because those whose professional fate will depend on the Minister of Justice will have priority in obtaining the status of judge. The best proof of this is the introduction of the principle that those who hold the positions of the judicial assessor will not have to take part in competitions for a free judicial position, while the competition road will be the only one for referenda, assistants of judges, prosecutors or professionals. This is another violation of the Constitution, namely its Article. 60, according to which Polish citizens who exercise their full public rights have the right to access public service on an equal basis. Naturally, therefore, fear and doubt arise as to the true intention of this solution. A small proposal to remove from the composition of the examination committee, in which the majority have representatives of the Ministry of Justice, the only representative of the National Judicial Board, is also a certain addition to the above amendments. Changes to the Programme Board should also be critically assessed. The Act assumes that it is not her chairman chosen by the Council itself from among the members, and that the head of the national school will have a real impact on the management of the work of the Programme Board. Let me just add that the head of the national school is, of course, appointed and cancelled by the Minister of Justice. In addition, legal authorities are excluded from the composition of the professional council and the participation of representatives of the National Courts of Justice is limited. The project also deprives the Council of its direct impact on the development of timetables for school activities, the adoption of application programmes, approval of the annual report on school activities, as well as the possibility of submitting candidates for lecturers. Practically all the competences of the board have been limited and its previous competences have been entrusted to the school's principal. In order to sum up this bill, the following description can be made: the organisation of the school and the training of applicants will take place in a manner determined by the persons appointed solely by the Minister of Justice. The examination committee, also fully subordinate to the Minister, will decide on the future of the professional graduates of the school as assessors. Then, instead of the President, the Minister will entrust the duties of judge and take the oath, and ultimately they will be entitled to the status of judge in the first place. Let me remind you that the same Minister of Justice is also the head of all prosecutors in Poland. Supplementary recruitment would be carried out from among those who practiced for an appropriate period in other legal professions. These are the people who prepare the projects for judicial decisions, resolutions, judgments, and the referees even issue decisions in their respective fields of competence. As we can read in the opinion, [[♪ The bell ♪]] that period may be too short and constitute an illusory entitlement. Last sentence, Marshal. It is an organ which in the constitution has been given an extremely cheap term, so that it is He called, namely, the constitutional legislature gave the ordinary legislature the issues concerning not only the mode of work, how to appoint the members of the National Judicial Council, but also the system or finally the scope of the action, i.e. four elements: the scope, the system, the method of appointment of members and the mode of work, to be defined by the ordinary legislature. In view of the above, the legislator has stated that it is necessary to move away from the corporate model of appointing judges, which is inefficient, at the moment. There is a departure from the model in which the judges refer themselves to the National Council of Courts, decide on their promotion, they decide on their own assessment, because it is highly inefficient. It has led to a situation in which we are dealing with a model where the judge himself is not responsible for the actions of the judiciary, where there is no clear distribution of powers and responsibilities. This model is highly inefficient, and in every respect, both from the point of view of disciplinary proceedings, transparency, transparency, objectivity of justice and inefficiencies in the context of proceedings, the chronicity of these cases. Therefore, both the appointment of the members of the National Judicial Board and the mode of work and the scope of action must be changed. How? There's only one way to ensure that we move away from the corporationry of a judge. In democracy no better model has been found than social control over the appointment of judges, the appointment of members to the National Judicial Council. We don't go as far as that. in the German model. Last week I had the pleasure of visiting Bavaria, where the Minister of Justice decides directly to appoint a judge, i.e. the executive authority directly appoints judges. We're just proposing an intermediate model. Elections to the National Judicial Council for elected members of judges will be held by Parliament. This model will ensure that representatives of society with a democratic mandate will have an impact on the appointment of these members. As regards mode of work, we propose two meetings in the name of the principle of control and balance of the three authorities referred to in Article. 10. So, significantly, the judge, who today, let's face it, unfortunately, is in the current model, is changing to democracy. It is precisely the control and balance of the three authorities that we aim for by introducing two assemblies, leading to the situation that the voice of the representatives of society with a democratic mandate will not be a fig leaf, as so far it has been the case that the judges who were elected from among the judges had a powerful decisive influence on the decisions taken by that authority. As regards the third issue, the expiry of the mandate, this is also where the constitutional legislature left it to the ordinary legislature. I would like to remind you that in 2001 we had seven cases of expiry of the mandate in accordance with the KRS Act of 2001. In 2011, the ordinary legislature introduced six such conditions. In its judgment K 25/07, the Constitutional Tribunal stated that the expiry of the mandate was admissible for material reasons. The reasons that we are introducing at the moment are, firstly, the fact that the issue of changing the organisation and way of work and the choice of members of the KRS requires a decisive change in terms of terms of terms. It is a term of office, which has so far been individual, since the position of the 2002 National Court of Justice, which was taken, and let's face it, in violation of constitutional principles, because the National Court of Justice had no right to correct by means of its Constitutional Resolution, and so it happened in 2002, introduced individual terms, when only members of the Constitutional Court, the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal, had individual terms. So that we eliminate these terms of office, we eliminate the state of non-compliance with the Constitution in this respect, and it is necessary for this to expire the mandate. Finally, the curian elections, which were also questioned, by the position of the general assembly judges themselves. A few years ago, the General Assembly adopted a resolution indicating that the rules governing the election to the National Council of Judicial Affairs on the basis of curia, i.e. non-democratic, i.e. non-universal, indirect, violate the Constitution. Thus, those who are currently sitting on the National Judicial Council sit there in a way that violates the Constitution. It is therefore necessary to introduce a further condition for the expiry of the mandate. The term of office is not an absolute principle because of the fact that in Article. 187 of the Constitution clearly states that in terms of mode of work, and precisely in the mode of work, there is a constitutionality issue, which is clear from the opinions ordered by the Office for Sejm Analysis, the term of office is the definition of the mode of work, and this can be defined by the ordinary legislature. If this were not the case, the six current conditions for the termination of the mandate would have to be considered unconstitutional, and it is not. It would have to be considered that the introduction of new rules for exclusivity of the mandate in the past, in the form of a ban on the appointment of a member of the National Court Register, at the same time as the President of the Tribunal, was incompatible with the Constitution, and that was not the case. Therefore, in this case, it is necessary to state that the mandates of the current members of the KRS are extinguished, for the reasons of the system, which I mentioned earlier. These changes will ensure transparent and democratic, direct and universal rules of choice for the National Judicial Council. Any kind of thesis on the politicisation of the members of the KRS is unfounded, as - let's not forget - elections will be made from among the judges themselves. It's a myth. The independence of judges will be guaranteed in a much stronger way than it is today. The judges are independent - and will be independent - but they are not independent of the state, they are not independent of the nation, from the Republic, because according to the article. 4 society, the nation exercises power directly or indirectly, there is no third possibility. Therefore, in the name of balancing and control of the authorities, in the name of the sovereignty of the nation, in the name of the sovereignty of the nation and finally in the name of the principle that judges rule on behalf of the Republic of Poland, these changes are necessary. Judges do not rule on behalf of their colleagues - they rule on behalf of the State, on behalf of society, on behalf of all citizens, and citizens should have an influence, even minimally, on who and how they become judges. They don't have that today. These changes are therefore necessary. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

This is, High Izbo, the theme of the project that I have the honour of presenting today to the High House. The National Judicial Council is a specific constitutional body upholding the independence of the courts and independence of judges, having also the right to apply to the Constitutional Court for a proposal to comply with the Constitution of acts in so far as they concern the independence of the courts and independence of judges, and presenting applications to the President of the Republic of Poland for appointment to serve as a judge. The draft law amending the Act on the National Council of Courts comprehensively regulates the procedure for the selection of members to the National Courts by Supreme Court judges, general courts judges, Supreme Administrative Court judges, administrative courts judges and military courts judges. Constitution of the Republic of Poland in art. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 3 provides for the term of office of the elected members of the National Judicial Board. The elected members are 21 members, including 15 judges, four Members and two Senators - Article. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 points 2 and 3 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The proposed amendment re-establishes the number of members of the board elected by the judges of the various courts. It's an art, actually. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 point 2 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland provides for 15 judges elected by judges from among the judges of the Supreme Court, universal courts, administrative courts and military courts. The aim of the proposed amendment is to implement the principles of judicial self-government by introducing a new, fully democratic model for the election of judges' representatives to the constitutional body upholding the independence of courts and the independence of judges. The aim of the proposed amendment is also to increase the transparency of the choices made and, as important and also very often requested by social organisations, the participation of the public, including professional authorities, thereby increasing confidence in the Council as a guarantee of the independence of courts from the influence of politicians. Increasing transparency and public participation in the election of members of the Council will be implemented by, first, extending the circle of bodies entitled to submit candidates for members of the Board, that is the new wording of the Article. Article 12 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 8,10, secondly, mandatory public hearing of candidates with the possibility of citizens asking questions at least one week before the elections and - what is important - broadcast via the Internet. The extension of the group of entities entitled to submit candidates for members of the Board assumes that not only judges but also a group of at least 2 000 will be able to submit applications. citizens entitled to be elected to the Sejm, as well as the Ombudsman, the General Bar Council, the National Council of Legal Counsels, the National Council of Prosecutors at the Attorney General, and the Council of the Public University Law Faculty. Such a specific catalogue of bodies entitled to submit candidates will provide the new National Judicial Council with democracy and pluralism. The mandatory public hearing and the possibility of asking questions to candidates for members of the Board refer to the informal civil institution for monitoring candidates for judges of the Constitutional Tribunal and the Prosecutor General. It is appropriate to formalise this institution so that the public can get to know all the candidates and confront them with the outcome of the elections. First of all, it should be stressed that a mandatory public hearing will contribute to the transparency of the election of the members of the National Courts Council. The project also envisages a change in the way elections are conducted. The selection of judges to the Council will be carried out by judges from among the candidates submitted by the eligible actors in democratic and direct elections preceded by a public hearing. The choice of judges to the National Judicial Council should be made by judges, as this is a European standard. The opinion of the Executive Board of the European Judicial Council Network on draft laws presented by the Polish Government, dated 30 January 2017, should be recalled here. It stresses that the European Network of Judicial Councils has clear standards in this respect, which state that the mechanism for appointing the members of the Board elected from among judges must be such a mechanism as to exclude interference by the executive or legislative authority. The European Judicial Council Network also stresses that the mechanism for appointing the members of the board elected from among judges must exclude interference by the executive or legislative authority and that the choice of judges should only be made by other judges on the basis of a broad representation of the relevant sectors of judicial authority. High Izbo! The project therefore implements the European standard for the selection of similar bodies in the judicial system. It is the judges themselves who determine the composition of the KRS counterparts in other countries. This is the norm in a civilized world, not the choice of members of judicial councils by politicians. The proposed law envisages the introduction of parities for the number of seats filled by judges of individual courts, so that judges of each level of the judiciary elect members of the Council in full democratic and direct elections. According to the proposed new wording of the article. 11 the Council will be elected: one Supreme Court judge among the judges of the Supreme Court, two judges of the Supreme Court, one Supreme Administrative Court judge among the judges of that court, one regional administrative court judge among the judges of those courts, so far the assembly of regional administrative courts and the Supreme Administrative Court elected two members of the Board, one military court judge elected from among the judges of those courts, i.e. eight general court judges elected by regional courts, two general court judges elected by district courts, one general court judge elected by judges of appeal. These parities shall take account of the representation in the Board of Judges of each type of court and their size shall be based on the number of judges in the courts concerned, assuming that each type of court should have at least one representative in the Council. These are: one Supreme Court judge for about 79 judges, one Supreme Administrative Court judge for about 109 Supreme Administrative Court judges, one Provincial Administrative Court judge for about 530 regional administrative courts judges, one military court judge for about 58 military judges, eight judges elected by about 7,000. regional court judges out of about 10,000. general court judges, two judges elected by around 2600 district courts out of about 10 000. General court judges, one judge elected by ca. 512 judges of appeal courts out of ca. 10 000. general court judges. Designed art. Article 11a (1) (a) (ii) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: The President of the courts competent to hold elections and the appointment of electoral committees shall be designated. It is assumed that there will be no need to create electoral districts within the courts of different types and that elections will be made on one national list. This solution guarantees representation in the National Courts of Justice for all categories of judges and ensures pluralism of the opinions of the members of the National Courts Council. The draft also provides for the establishment of a five-member electoral commission, its chairman and the appointment of an election date. The obligation imposed by the law shall be exercised in person by the President of the competent court by means of an order, the first President of the Supreme Court in respect of ordinary courts. The President shall also be responsible for preparing and handing out the ballot cards as laid down by the Minister of Justice's Regulation to the Presidents of the courts in which the elections will take place. Focusing these powers in the hands of one person will ensure efficient and swift decision-making in the course of the elections. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 point 2 of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. Art. Point 5 of the draft assumes that voting in elections will be secret and direct. The project assumes that the election will be won by the candidate with the highest number of votes and, in the event of an equal number of votes, by a senior judge. The proposed solution is intended to prevent costly re-election process, assuming that equal numbers of votes are unlikely due to the number of voters. In the election, there will be as many candidates as there are cast. The vote will take place at the courts' offices, which will ensure their undistorted work. The judge's statutory duty will be to take part in the election. The President of the court at whose seat the vote will take place will ensure its proper conduct, that is, he will draw up a list of the judges voting in the elections, issue the ballot cards, allow the vote to be kept confidential and will pass after the election the votes cast, the unused ballots and the voting lists of the judges. The electoral commission appointed by the President of the competent court shall, within three days of the votes cast, draw up a electoral protocol showing the number of votes cast for each candidate and the number of votes void. The election committee will also be responsible, in accordance with the proposed provision, for public announcement of the results of the elections by means of a notice published on the website of the competent court. Designed art. 13a of the Act introduces a wide-ranging electoral control mechanism to which every judge will be entitled. The election control by the Supreme Court, including the competence to make a decision to repeat the elections, will provide sufficient safeguards for the regularity of the elections. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u366	Mr. Marshal! Gentlemen of Ministers! High Izbo! In a democratic legal state, the constitutional value defining the identity of the system is the independence of the courts and independence of the judges, and thus the independence of the National Judicial Council, which is of particular importance to guarantee the principle of division and balance of the authorities and the right to the court. Art. 173 of the Constitution clearly separates judicial authority from other authorities, assuming that it constitutes an independent whole. Thus, the principle of the division and balance of the authorities as set out in Article. The 10th Constitution should be understood with regard to the judicial authority in such a way that its separation and independence are duly respected. This means that the interference of the legislative and executive authorities in the sphere of influence of the judicial authority is only possible in the constitutionally acceptable scope. Art. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 4 of the Constitution provides that the system, scope and mode of operation of the National Judicial Council and the way in which its members are elected shall be governed by the law. However, the legislator may not amend the provisions set out in Article. 187 Constitutions of the statutory functions of the Council in such a way that it strengthens the role of the out-of-court factor bypassing the Constitution. The Constitution's creators were aware of the existence of such risks and were therefore in favour of including many detailed rules on the composition of the Council in constitutional matters. The entry into force of the proposed amendment, which goes far beyond the constitutionally permitted regulatory freedom of the legislator, will have the effect of politicalising both the choice and the day-to-day functioning of the NRA. The degree of risk regarding the politicisation of the National Courts, i.e. the departure from the standards of the close judicial authorities, is in this case all the more serious because the term of office of the elected members of the National Courts will coincide with the term of office in the Sejm. The proposed change goes so far that it not only deprives the community of judges of the choice of their representatives to the National Courts of Justice, but in general the possibility of presenting candidates for members of the National Courts. Designed art. Article 11 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 provides that candidates for members of the National Court Register shall be presented to the Marshal of the Sejm Bureau or to a group of at least 50 Members. According to the draft law, only associations of judges may submit recommendations to the Marshal of the Sejm concerning the application of candidates for members of the Board. What if the candidate is applied on the last day and there's no time for an opinion? In the light of the Constitution, it is a choice made among judges by judges, not by the Sejm, the President or the Council of Ministers. The termination of the term of office of the elected members of the Board by law would mean a disproportionate interference of the legislator in a constitutionally shaped system for the election and functioning of the National Judicial Council. Shortening the term of office of elected members would also mean introducing a simple amendment to the Constitution without amending the Article. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 3 Constitutions. In case the president doesn't like it, for example, because it's going to be another option, will the majority of the parliament of the ordinary law shorten its term of office? After all, what the PiS proposes can be applied to any body that has a specific term of office. Art. 187th Constitution excludes the division of the council into assemblies. There is no doubt in the doctrine that the mixed nature of the composition of the council does not mean its internal division into hens or teams. The KRS shall act as a single collegial body. All its members shall have the same position in its composition, and in particular the same voting rights. Since the division into two assemblies is not in accordance with the Constitution, this non-compliance cannot remove the proposed establishment of a kind of super assembly composed exclusively of judges and the sovereign to decide subject to unanimity, the dispute between the assemblies. The proposed law contains a solution which changes without prior amendment of the Constitution both the state position of the National Judicial Council and the state position of the President. The Constitution shall be laid down in Article. 179 that the President appoints judges at the request of the National Judicial Council, which does not mean making a choice. In the light of Art. 179 in the Constitution, the powers of the President are reduced by the appointment or refusal to appoint a judge in accordance with the proposal of the Council, and the powers of the Council are reduced by the formulation of a single request for appointment, not by a number of proposals. In carrying out the disposition contained in the proposal, the Council should at the same time present to the President a request for the appointment and failure to appoint the same person as a judge, because the position of the President is not known to the Council before the decision expressed in the proposal. The draft essentially weakens the Council's position, without a proposal that is currently impossible to become a judge. At the same time, it strengthens the role of the head of state in the appointing of judges, in breach of the balance between the judicial authority and the executive authority. The draft amendment to the position of the National Judicial Council is incompatible with the principle of ensuring the integrity and efficiency of public institutions, as expressed in the preamble to the Constitution. The adoption of the proposed law will change the relationship between the legislative authority and the judicial authority as defined in the Constitution, replacing the existing advantage of judges in the National Council of Judicial Affairs with a political factor, and may also contribute to strengthening the role of executive president. Thus, the Modern requests that the project be rejected at first reading. This project cannot simply be improved. On the other hand, we are in favour of a project developed by Iustitia, supported by modern clubs, Civic Platform and PSL. It proposes a wide group of entities entitled to apply, for example, for a group of 2 000, ladies and gentlemen. citizens, but also a mandatory public hearing or a requirement to announce a list of candidates at least one week before the elections. It shall also maintain the independence of the KRS from the executive and legislative authority. It provides a transparent procedure for the selection of judges to the National Judicial Council. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u374	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the government proposal for a law amending the Act on the National Council of Courts and certain other laws and on the draft law amending the Act on the National Council of Courts and on certain other laws. As we can read in the explanatory memorandum, the Government's draft law covers the rules governing the selection of members of the National Judicial Council from among judges, introduces new rules for the National Judicial Council to assess candidates for judicial and judicial positions and provides for the creation of new bodies of the National Judicial Council for this purpose. In fact, the parliamentary bill drawn up by the Association of Judges Iustitia focuses solely on the procedure for the selection of judges to the National Courts of Justice. At this point, it is worth noting, regardless of the views expressed on the functioning of the judiciary, that the complicated procedure for the selection of judges to the National Judicial Council, which is not of the nature of direct elections, which makes it impossible, in fact, to represent the most numerous group of judges of the regional courts in the Council, to change without delay. The difference is what this change is supposed to look like. The government project provides for the selection of candidates for board members by the Sejm of the Republic. Candidates would be presented to the High House by the marshal on the basis of reports made by the Sejm Bureau or by a group of at least 50 Members. This solution seems incompatible with the current wording of the Article. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 Constitution of the Republic of Poland relating to the composition of the National Courts of Justice. Point 3 of that provision gives the Sejm a clear competence to elect four members of the National Judicial Council from among Members, similar to the selection of 15 judges of the Constitutional Tribunal and 16 members of the Court of State. Such a provision is not explicitly provided for in point 2 concerning the election of 15 members elected from the Supreme Court judges, universal courts, administrative courts and military courts to the National Judicial Board. This choice should be made by the judges in the light of the judgment of the Constitutional Court of 18 July 2007. K 25/07, namely point 4 of the statement of reasons: Article. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. Point 1 (2) of the Constitution establishes the principle of the election of judges to the National Judicial Council. This provision provides that judges may be members of the National Council of Courts without indicating any additional features which would condition membership of the Board. The selection shall be made from among the four listed in Article. Article 187 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. Point 1 (2) of the Constitution of the groups of judges. There is no possibility of their cancellation, indicating their four-year term. Nor has the legislator introduced a restriction so that either of the judges could not be elected or could not be elected to the National Judicial Council. In this context, the wording of the article is therefore debatable. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the government project submitted, which shall expire the term of office of the current members of the National Judicial Council. The Free and Solidarnia Circle states that the selection of members of the National Judicial Council from among judges should be made by all judges in direct, democratic elections, in which each judge can take part. In the election of 15 judges of the members of the National Judicial Council, each judge should have an active and passive electoral right, with a parity of representation of the general courts, administrative and military courts and the Supreme Court. The rules of choice should allow for the selection of more than the current number of judges of regional courts, which are the most important in the judicial community. Therefore, they should be properly represented in the Council. At the same time, we consider that it is in line with the law that the basic arrangements allow for balancing the votes of members of judges with the votes of members from bodies from the general election, i.e. representatives of the Sejm, the Senate and the President of the Republic. On the other hand, with regard to the project submitted by representatives of the PO, Modern and PSL, we believe that it regulates the issues necessary to amend too narrowly. Unlike the government project, there is no regulation regarding the procedure for the nomination of candidates for judges, which in our assessment requires a change. The solutions proposed in the government project, as well as in the parliamentary project, according to the Free and Solidarnia Circle, are underdeveloped and require wider discussion and consultation, [[♪ The bell ♪]] as well as extensive consultation with the judicial community. Further work on the government project is, in our view, possible, and we therefore request that it be directed towards further legislative work. On the other hand, the parliamentary project as an incomplete, non-regulatory nomination of judges and as a de facto voice in its own matters of the judicial environment does not deserve our support. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u436	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The Commission dealt with the draft Act on statutory auditors, audit firms and public oversight. She set up an extraordinary subcommittee for this purpose. The subcommittee worked from December to March, held 10 meetings, during which we had the opportunity to hear the opinion of all interested in this draft law. And the project is important. The proposal deals with regulations relating in particular to this professional group, which are auditors, which are auditors, and for them it is, you can say, a constitution. But it also concerns the entire market, which is based on the credibility of these financial statements and those data that we receive thanks to the independence and reliability of the auditors' work. We listened to a debate that took place during the work of the Parliament's subcommittee and during the work of the committee, as well as in the public space where there was a discussion between supporters of various solutions, because in this project, a government project, we implement European law, adapting it to Polish realities. And these Polish realities are quite specific and require some state intervention, which is more radical than in other countries. I mean, we have this market of services in this area, audit services, which is a market dominated by the largest, so-called. a large four, entities which, according to the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection, significantly establish oligopolies in this market, and this market is largely closed to entities smaller than those with a dominant position which is clear. Any intervention that will defocus this market is desirable. It was also during the implementation of these provisions that both the Minister and all the Members who worked on this project were behind the head. The whole idea to redefine audit and advice on the capital market not only in Poland but all over the world, from the United States to Europe, of course, resulted from the crisis, from the reflections that were connected with what happened. There has been a very serious discussion and, as a result of this discussion, European legislation has in a sense forced the Member States of the European Union to adopt a whole range of solutions that regulate this market in a much more rigorous way than it did before. We talked about this and from this tribune, I also talked about it, everyone knows it from public messages, so I will not mention these examples in the world and in Europe. On the other hand, the result of this reflection is the European rules which we introduce into the Polish legal order in this draft law. What's driving us? Above all, we are concerned about the independence of the auditor and his professional skepticism, which is an overriding value. That is, we believe that what we receive as market participants, as all potentially and really interested in this information from auditors, is so important for the entire economy that this independence of the auditor should be protected to the maximum extent possible. And these radical, as many will probably admit, the solutions we have adopted and which we are proposing to the High House are therefore so firm. That is to say, we want to resolve clearly the separation of all services provided by auditors other than financial audit services and to lead to a situation in which no one is a judge in their own case, i.e. in which we guarantee that auditors, doing their job, at the work of the one which is, as I said, extremely important for the entire market, are completely independent, also within the framework of those large audit and advisory companies which today have a dominant position on the Polish market, in particular as regards listed companies. The second area, which is equally important, is the standard of these tests, their quality, their readability for the entire market. It's something that's very important. And here, together with the board of auditors, together with the environment of these auditors, we are responsible for this and regulate it in the provisions of this law. Finally, the third thing, that is our responsibility, the responsibility of the state. That is, if we expect auditors to have independence, if we consider this independence to be the highest value if we re-establish regulations within the statutory authority, we also create an institutional framework for the State's responsibility for this process, for the financial review process, and for the credibility of the information that the market receives. Therefore, the role of the KNA, which is very, very important here, and the role of the State, also the role of the State in terms of sanctions for violating this law, which are important, i.e. which are intended to make it impossible for someone to be tempted not to comply with this chapter we are talking about here. During the work on the committee, of course, we focused on a number of issues and, it can be said, legislative and editorial, and the substantive amendments were far-reaching, and finally there was a government amendment, which clearly determined the outcome of our discussions on the fundamental issue of separation of audit advice, but it can be said that they were around rotation, i.e. the frequency imposed by the law and limited to a certain period, a relatively short period, as for European conditions. This is the thing that we had behind our heads, this public demand for deconcentration, the opening of this market also for Polish medium-sized entities. We'll see if it works, but we'll force a faster rotation of audit firms. We have identified these companies, which are the most important for the market in Poland. We called them individuals of public interest. We have defined which companies, units belong to this JZP directory. To them, these rigors are much greater than those of the others. They are most important for the economy, and therefore these rigors are much more demanding. And finally, sanctions. It's not the sword that's in the vagina, it's the sword that can be removed. Indeed, these penalties are potentially severe for those who do not. These rules are for the common good, because they serve precisely to make this Polish market a credible market for all its participants. It was very bad when working on this law that it was treated by the public as part of the rivalry of two professional groups, namely auditors and tax advisors. It's not true, it's just not the right picture. This, of course, looks attractive from the point of view of the media, because it is easy to present a thing as a rivalry of different professional groups. As we considered that from the market as a whole the most important values are the credibility, independence and professional scepticism of the auditor, we came to the conclusion that the situation where the auditor is in a wider group providing services other than audit services could potentially affect the independence of the auditor. You can say that in the case of auditors we create maximum health work. The conditions that auditors in this new law have are far more comfortable conditions for their work than those they have so far had. The aim was only to care for the entire market. In this market, everyone has their jobs. Other are the tasks of auditors, others are the tasks of tax advisors. Separation of these services serves all, hence our conviction that this is the way to judge. We've finished our work in the subcommittee. The committee's report is the subject of our deliberations today. This is a good project, a good result of our work. I would like to thank all those who have joined this work, including the social side, the Legislative Bureau. I think we have developed a good solution that will enable the independent development of both the audit services market and the advisory services market, while at the same time giving all participants of this market the assurance that the information they receive is information about the highest possible standard, and the Polish state will take responsibility for enforcing the implementation of this law. ParlaMint-PL_2017-04-05-sejm-39-1.u440	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club on the government bill on auditors, audit firms and public oversight, prints no. 1092 and 1447. The main objective of the proposed law is to implement into the Polish legal order the provisions of the new European Union audit legislation covering Directive 2014/56 of the European Union on statutory audits of annual accounts and consolidated accounts, Regulation 537/2014 on detailed requirements for statutory audits of financial statements of entities of public interest. During the legislative work, in particular during the session of the extraordinary subcommittee, a lot of time was devoted to examining nearly a thousand legislative amendments tabled by the Legislative Bureau. This made the work of the subcommittee very difficult and limited the possibilities of substantive work. The legislative quality of the government proposal raised serious concerns. During the discussion, some issues, in particular the catalogue of bodies defined as bodies of public interest, were highly controversial. The greatest reservations concerned the exclusion, at the request of Members of the Law and Justice, of the catalogue of entities of the National Credit and Credit Savings and cooperative savings and credit banks, even though the government included these entities in the first version of the project. Although, after reflection, there was an amendment restoring cooperative savings and credit banks to the catalogue of entities defined as entities of public interest, it is a mistake to abandon the national cash register in the current situation of those entities. For this reason, I submit on behalf of the Parliamentary Club the Civic Platform amendments restoring the condition of the first version of the draft Print Act No. 1092 with a certain modification. Another issue that raises serious doubts is the Government's amendment extending in Article. 136 a catalogue of services prohibited from companies examining financial statements for other services other than auditing activities. The introduction of a complete ban on the provision of ancillary services may lead to a situation preventing a statutory audit. Such an absolute limitation will lead to complications in the provision of audit services at the level of international capital groups. As stated in the discussion, this provision will significantly impede the functioning of the cooperative bank sector, as the auditing unions examining the financial statements of cooperative banks will not be able to carry out the vetting in those banks under the Cooperative Law. I understand the purpose of introducing a list of prohibited services, but I believe that it had to be balanced whether such a provision would not introduce much more complications and difficulties than would bring benefits. I would like to stress that it is legitimate for us to introduce restrictions on the possibility of providing additional services to the audited public interest entity, but the introduction of a complete ban is a mistake. In the final part of my speech, I would like to draw attention to the objectives pursued by Community rules, which we are implementing in the Polish legal system. Firstly, it is a strengthening of the independence and objectivity of statutory auditors and audit firms. Secondly, improving the quality of statutory audits by better quality reporting from research to different addressees. Thirdly, strengthening public oversight by strengthening its independence from the environment of auditors. It is important, what I stress with all my power, that these objectives should be achieved under Polish conditions. Thank you for your attention. These are corrections, Mr. Marshal.

They were primarily intended to clarify the conditions under which such documents were produced and thus to give professional scepticism to experts, to protect their independence and to meet such requirements that the auditor would be the person who would be credible - it would be credible in relation to the needs of owners, executives of companies, entities, but also future owners, people who make decisions, and they often take them very quickly, often by means of stock-exchange decisions on the acquisition of assets, shares, being owners of the portfolio bought by intermediaries, by investment fund associations. So that someone could predict that Katyń would again demand Polish blood, that this flight would end in a terrible catastrophe, the greatest national tragedy in the postwar history of Poland. 96 people died in it, including my sleep. brother, president of Poland Lech Kaczyński, my sleep. sister-in-law Maria Kaczyńska and many of my friends. They're all down at the station. Death in the service of their homeland brought them together with their predecessors - those who fought for Poland and worked for Poland during our history. With all my heart I thank all those who contributed to bringing the land from Katyn and Smolensk, the creation of a memorial plaque and the celebration of today. As a member of this land, I also thank the school director, Ms.Ewie Adamczyk, and the teachers, for this valuable initiative shaping the attitudes of children and young people, building love for their homeland, respecting history and indicating authority. It is important that the Polish school also raises young patriots in addition to teaching. The School of the Association of Friends of Catholic Schools in Warzachewka Polska raises its students in a manner worthy of imitation. Thank you. [[Applause]]

On those days when we celebrate the 77th anniversary of the Katyn crime, it seems that the entire mystery around this crime is explained, and that is not true. Many secrets were taken with him to the grave and to the NKVD archives by a man named Zarubun, who interrogated our soldiers in the Koziel camp. In his further intelligence career, he seemed to have played two very important roles: one very important for the fate of World War II and the other very important for us. And this second role is his action, which, it seems, is probably still going on today. The first role is spring 1941, where it suddenly turns out that Stalin, who is informed from all over the world that there will be a German-Russian war, does not take any practical action except one that sends Zarubina and another NKVD officer to Yugoslavia. The result of this visit is the war between Yugoslavia and Germany, i.e. the attack on Soviet Russia is postponed by 2 and 3 months. What are the consequences of this, let us leave the historians to consider. I would like to talk about another aspect of his activity. A year and a half after his involvement in the Katyn crime, this criminal was appointed by another criminal named Joseph Stalin as head of the NKVD delegation in Washington. The effects of his work have been published today by the U.S. services, specifically the FBI, in volumes which are defined in a single word - Venona, Venony Act. This man organized a nuclear network, a spy network, and as a result the Soviets quickly acquired this weapon and became a nuclear power, which caused history to turn out the way it did. On the other hand, it is not said that in the autumn of 1941, the important task of Zarubin was to influence through agents of influence, e.g. by Mr Bolesław Gebert, but about him at another time, and other activists of the so-called. The Communist Party of the United States to the American elite at the time, so that there would be no agreement between the United States and the Nazi Germans, of course, such an agreement that would be detrimental to the Soviet Union. In fact, it was possible to say that the catalogue, although very, very shortened, of this gentleman's actions is already closed. Nothing more wrong. Since October 1941, when he appears, of course under a changed name, in the United States, he has been a very active participant in various activities of Poles living in the United States.
